ParlaMint-AT_2021-02-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00085_d7e10332	Mr President. Mrs. Minister! High house! In the form of the current government template, they are quoted as follows: Tina Blau, Gerhard Bronner, Sigmund Freud, Hugo von Hofmannsthal, Marie Jahoda, Hans Kelsen, Hedy Lamarr, Gustav Mahler, Ludwig von Mises, Marcel Prawy, Max Reinhardt, Arthur Schnitzler, Arnold Schönberg, Friedrich Torberg, to name a few. A few years ago, the renowned British weekly magazine “Economist” Vienna became the city of the last century. It was an incredible collection of genius, mind, talent, ingenuity, entrepreneurship. Vienna produced personalities who have lastingly shaped the world of music, art, literature, entrepreneurship, humanities and natural sciences, not only those of Vienna, but the whole world. Above average, many of these personalities were Austrian Jews. They have left clear traces in the history of Austria, they have contributed substantially to what we feel today is typical Austrian. But it was not only the celebrities, the stars, there were countless craftsmen, workers who contributed to Austria – as loyal Austrians in the monarchy, but also as founding fathers of the newly founded Republic. For example, the Jewish community also performed an above-average blood duty in the First World War in the service of the K.-u.-k. army. The current government proposal is directed towards the future. It serves first of all to ensure an active Jewish community life in Austria, the broad access of the population of Austria to the Jewish cultural heritage, to the expansion of interreligious dialogue and to ensure the active participation of young Jews in the Jewish community. The wise former president of the Israeli Cultural Community, Hofrat Paul Grosz, once said in a discussion: Look at how the Jewish community is, then you know how the country is going! – This has only proved true too often. So how is the Jewish community in Austria going today, and what are the future challenges? – Yes, there is the threat of anti-Semitism. On the other hand, there is a decline in traditional anti-Semitism, but unfortunately also an increase in a new anti-Semitism, especially in Muslim communities. The terrorist attacks are a good example. Here anti-Semitism mixes with the aim of attacking our Western community of values, with our common values: Democracy, rule of law, equality, cultural and religious diversity. Ensuring the security of its citizens is one of the most elementary tasks of a rule of law, and this, of course, also applies to the Jewish community. It is faced with the perception of many security tasks on a high standard, which are covered by the current draft law. How is the Jewish community today in Austria? – It is a small – currently about 15,000 Jewish Austrians live in Austria – but a very lively community. There are numerous synagogues, houses, Jewish kindergartens and schools, kosher supermarkets, restaurants, the Jewish Museum and so on. The law is directed towards the future, it helps the Jewish community to further rebuild and maintain the community with the necessary security measures, but it also acts as a supporter in youth work, in the development of interreligious and intercultural exchange and so on. The Federal Government has achieved an important and good throw with this law. A great deal of thanks goes to Federal Chancellor Kurz and, in particular, to Mrs. Karoline Edtstadler. I was very happy to see in the committee that this draft law will receive the unanimous approval of all parties. This is a good, an important, a worthy sign. Thank you.

They were then so-called mixings of first or second degrees, although they wanted nothing more to do with Judaism. In May 1945, a handful of Jews lived in Austria – 60,000 were murdered, the rest could flee, a few survived as submarines in Austria. Among other things, the couple Ella and Ludwig Weisz were able to escape. ==References== District used to have a small radio business, which was very prosperous, because the radio in the 1930s was the source of information. Right in March 1938, the business was arranged and gradually other parts of the business, namely the entire inventory; then the apartment was arranged, and finally, in April 1939, the flight to Palestine succeeded. Ludwig Weiz then says: “We have had to live, not, but [...] just to live”. They couldn't afford an apartment, but they stayed Vienna. They wanted to return again and again, and hardly was the war over, hardly was the Holocaust over, they wanted to go back to Vienna. You will then also write to Vienna again and again: We want to go back, how's it going? – They need an entry permit, as Wiener and Wienerin an entry permit from Palestine to her hometown. It was not given to them. It lasted until April 1947 – exactly eight years after they had fled – that they could return to Vienna. The apartment didn't get them back, the business didn't get them back. They could then start a new business, but it wasn't all. It wasn't like this anymore: We've had no way of starting back where we left off. Now you're wondering: Why did they want to return to this country from which they were expelled, where others, their friends, friends, their families, were murdered? – Ella Weisz says in an interview: “We were Jews, but we were Vienna.” You've always had homesickness. After 1945, Austria was unable to recognize that it was precisely these survivors who had survived that they should be prepared for a friendly reception here. Late, very late, we now have a bill to ensure: that Jewish life – there are about 10,000 Jews, Jews living in Austria – is ensured that we guarantee the security of cultural exchange and that we really have a Jewish community where the people who live here are doing well. ParlaMint-AT_2021-02-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00085_d7e10992	Madam President. Mr. Vice-Chancellor! Ladies and gentlemen, Ladies and gentlemen! The President-in-Office has already mentioned this: We are now dealing with the tender law, a law that hardly affects the general population. Appreciate spectators in front of the screens! Most of you will never have heard of the existence of this law. It regulates the application for admission to the Federal Service and, accordingly, the corresponding admission procedure and the occupation of functions. From my point of view, it is important to know that not a single person makes these decisions, but a Commission, the so-called assessment committee, which is basically joint, namely, with two employee and two employer representatives. Currently, approximately 2 100 to 2 200 functions are occupied annually in the Federal Service under the Procurement Act. The amendments now before us are an editorial clarification indicating that the approved job descriptions must be the basis for the tender. This means that the required knowledge and skills of applicants must be identical to the job description. This expects further professionalization. It is expected that the tender procedure will be accelerated and that the call for proposals will be more transparent in terms of future job requirements. I welcome the unanimity in the Constitutional Committee and thank you very much for that. Ladies and gentlemen! Many staff members of the public service are particularly required in these months, I am now thinking of those who work in hospitals, old-age and nursing homes, universities, schools, kindergartens and the police, and I would like to thank everyone for their efforts. [[Congratulations on ÖVP and Greens.]] I would particularly like to mention today the employees who are working on contact tracking among the district headquarters and magistrates. They also do a special job in addition to their everyday work and are particularly required. They're making a job important to curbing the pandemic. I am very, very grateful to all those!

The rural family farms – and this is quite different from the agricultural models of other countries in Europe, including our neighbouring countries – are built entirely, quite strongly on land-based management, from the mountain regions to the valleys. We mainly rely on the rural family farms. Yes, there will probably always be a spot on this earth, where we can produce cheaper and faster than we can in Austria, because in many ways we have few favors through the mountain areas and through the disadvantaged areas. However, we rely – and this is the very clear commitment of this Federal Government in particular – on land management in all regions and, above all, also support small-scale Austrian agriculture to a superior extent, because we are convinced that it is our very, very strong strength that we do not rely on a few large farms, but on small-scale family farms. That will also be our way for the future. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and MEPs of the Greens.]] The Green Report 2020 also shows very clearly how the agricultural production of natural factors is dependent. We had a very dry, low-sleeping year in 2019. This, of course, also affects the quantities of crops, which, of course, also affects market prices and, last but not least, incomes. In many ways, these are external factors that contribute to this, but perhaps market economy is not always the very great strength of individuals. The income from agriculture and forestry amounted to around 28 000 euros per holding in 2019 and thus remained de facto unchanged compared to 2018. Positive The main factors in income development were better income from direct marketing, heurigen farming and also from farm holidays. This is probably also one of the good news that the Green Report brings with it. In particular, there are smaller farms, which have chosen these branches and have been able to achieve an increase in income. Furthermore, the higher pig prices, the higher quantities of crops in cereals, hen and oil fruits and the increase in public funds for the payment of agricultural environmental measures have had a positive impact. In our support programmes, we have placed a major emphasis on climate protection, and this is of course also a source of income in agriculture. The high level of damage caused by the birch beetles has a significant effect on lower prices of wood, as well as higher costs for animal purchases, feed, energy and maintenance. We recorded low producer prices in fruit and wine. This has to do with a very high harvest in 2019. Reduced prices in the beef sector also have a negative impact on income. The year 2019 was marked by very different developments in the different forms of operations. The highest income increase – 49 percent – was achieved by the finishing companies. Of course, it is also very important for us to see where there are declines and to counter them accordingly. It may also be possible to acknowledge in this circle that a 49 percent increase especially in finishing plants is quite remarkable. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and MEPs of the Greens.]] The spread of African swine fever in Asia has led to strong price increases in Europe due to the high import needs, especially from China, in 2019. For the year 2020 the situation has turned completely, now the pig industry has been experiencing exactly the opposite for months. We have seen a massive market collapse through cases of African swine fever in Germany. We have therefore also launched, on the initiative of the Ministry of Agriculture, a very urgent loss compensation for these indirectly affected farms in agriculture. We explicitly support the family businesses that are going through a very difficult time, especially because gastronomy and hotels are also closed and therefore a very essential market for agriculture is missing. Positively, in 2019, the development of mixed agricultural holdings with an income increase of 9 percent and in market fruit holdings with 4 percent more income than in 2018. After the drought in the spring, the grain harvest later escaped in May. Positively, the lower pests in the case of hackers and also the higher area of cultivation of sunflowers and oil pumpkins have impacted. In forestry, income reductions of 10 percent had to be observed. We have a large precipitation deficit in very long heat periods. This affects us almost all over Austria, especially in Lower Austria and Upper Austria. This has resulted in an increased attack of borque käferschadholz and, accordingly, a fall in the price of wood. The situation on the wood market is not only a very difficult situation in Austria, but also a difficult one throughout Central Europe. The shady situation is enormous, especially in Slovakia, the Czech Republic and Germany, and this is a massive impact on prices. In this respect, we already have solutions in place, which will really provide massive support to the very heavily affected forest farmers and will also cover them. We make a total of 350 million euros available. That's it. The largest investment package in the Austrian forest in the history of the Second Republic, and I think we can be very proud of that. I should like to thank Parliament once again for its support. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and MEPs of the Greens.]] These measures have been adopted since 1. To apply for Feber for the rural family farms, and we will now proceed very quickly, because every day we reforest, is a good day because it creates the basis for the next and next generations, so that they too can generate income from the forest. The forest is the most important air conditioning in Austria – you must not forget that either. In 2019, the feed farms also had a 7 percent income reduction due to higher material expenses and amortisations in the case of constant incomes. The strongest fall in income – 31 percent – had to take up permanent crops, mainly due to enormous price reductions compared to the previous year in fruit production and loss of production in the wine-growing sector. Many of the contributions made by the honourable Members can be disproved: It is claimed that we would only look at the larger farms and in some way promote and support them disproportionately. The Green Report shows that the biggest increase is in direct marketing, in the area of farm and farm holidays, and in agriculture, in particular, there is a significant increase in the number of farms. This has, as has been said, very much to do with precipitation and harvest. In all these very sensitive areas, we are working intensively to develop support measures and, above all, we are trying to find alternative outlets. The market is, of course, crucial to us. In the next few months, we will set some important accents to ensure better value added in the farms. At this point I can express a very, very great thanks to all farmers in this country. I believe that the year 2020 and the Corona crisis in particular have shown how important and system-relevant our agricultural production is in Austria. We have had to get protective materials, medications and many others – so far also to the vaccines – from everywhere. With regard to food, we can supply ourselves in all important areas. I believe that this is one of the most important factors in food security in this country, and for this it is due to our farmers' great thanks, which work 365 days a year, that our tables are covered, that our shelves are full and that we have a living rural area in particular. – Thank you very much.

They recall the fierce debates we had at Ceta, debates we had at TTIP and now also in the Mercosur Agreement. In fact, the Mercosur agreement needs to be looked more closely. I'd like to advance: We as the ÖVP make a clear commitment to trade. The world has become more active and global, and trade is also global. Austria needs international trade, we benefit greatly from the export of our products. Thousands of jobs depend on exports, and especially in the pandemic, our industry and our exports have proved how stabilizing they are as economic factors and how jobs can be secured. Exports are important for industry, exports are important for the medium-sized economy in Austria, but exports are also important for agriculture. We produce more food in some areas than we consume domestically, for example milk: We produce 160 percent milk; We need 100 percent ourselves, 60 percent we have to export. [[Abg. Schellhorn:... eh something wrong!]] We produce 140 percent beef and we also need to export this beef, and I can remind you: We also export organic food to a large extent, including organic rice and organic milk. For agricultural exports, organic milk is often a door opener for exports to Switzerland, Germany and also overseas. The agricultural sector is also interested in fair, balanced export possibilities. Our trade balance shows yes: We exported about 1.7 billion agricultural goods in 1990, now over 12 billion. We also bring prosperity to the rural families. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and MEPs of the Greens.]] However, the crucial point is that world trade must be fair, that it must be balanced for all partners and that global concerns must be taken into account. The pure exchange of goods is too little. Such agreements must meet labour, social and environmental standards nowadays, they must be sustainable and comply with climate change criteria. These are the real criticisms. Example: The Mercosur Agreement is the Paris Convention on Climate Change. A central point of the agreement is that the tropical and subtropical rainforest should not be odated. Brazil, as an essential part of this Mercosur Agreement, is not taking any care of it. You know, the tropical rainforest binds greenhouse gases like CO2 and releases oxygen. Since 1990 alone, some 400,000 square kilometres of tropical rainforest have been wooded, which is the area of Germany and the Netherlands together – unimaginable! In the previous year – i.e. during the period of the Paris climate agreement – 11 000 square kilometres of rainforest were burned and wooded in addition to international commitments in Brazil, which corresponds to the area of Upper Austria. At the same time, agricultural products will be produced on these land, which will then come to Austria and Europe under the Mercosur Agreement. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Double farmer.]] We cannot accept that! Beef and veal will be imported, poultry, sugar and ethanol will be imported, even honey will be imported. [[New call for tender Double builder]] We as farmers are opposed to this because, of course, it concerns agriculture because, on the one hand, the European Union, within the framework of the Green Deal, has increased the requirements for farmers even more. The Farm-to-Fork Strategy and Biodiversity Strategy bring additional requirements, and farmers do not understand why they have more requirements in Austria and at the same time come in to us food that does not meet these standards. It is therefore important that the Mercosur Agreement is not accepted in the present form, that we see how it can be fair. [[Interlocutory calls from the Members of Parliament Lightfried and Cornelia Ecker]] – Mr Leichfried, as employee representatives, you must also be interested in the fact that we have an export that is fair and fair, and that is what we are committed to. None of this is in a free trade agreement. The questions we are debating at the Free Trade Agreement concern two things: Do we want to reduce barriers between economic areas? - Yes, that's what we want as a liberal. – And: Do we want this trade to help make the world better on both sides? – Yes, we want to do that as a liberal. [[Congratulations with the NEOS.]] Let us look at the situation in agricultural products: In the case of beef, we speak in the case of exemption from customs duties of 1 per cent of European production, while Latin America employs a total of 2.2 per cent of the production in Europe – in the case of a simultaneous, decades-long decline in beef consumption. This means quite specifically that there is no great danger, namely that the South American beef will not suddenly flood the European market – because this is simply not the case with this Treaty – or that Europeans would like it at all. The same is the case in the case of chicken meat, and the same applies to almost all other agricultural products for which there are always quotas. But now we – we NEOS not, but all the other parties in the National Council – stand out here and say there must be no trade in agricultural products, and at the same time we are delighted when Austrian wine is sold in California, in the United States, we are delighted when our pigs and similar products are exported. That's definitely so. The question is – and this is the question we have to ask ourselves: Is that why we want to exclude trade per se? Do we want to turn globalisation back to zero and almost back to a small forest village? – This will simply not be the case for economics. What it needs is a trade with mechanisms. This is the criticism that we cannot approve NEOS of the current Mercosur Agreement. We think it is the right way to anchor the climate agreement in it. This is already part of it, there are already sufficient test mechanisms, the only thing that is missing is the consequence. So if I find that my trading partner does not comply with the rules I agree with in the Treaty, there must be a consequence. A logical consequence would be that the relief provided for in the Treaty would be suspended. This is not, by the way, something that we, as the smallest group in the Austrian National Council, think of, but that is actually the thinking of many European partners, and it would be the right way. We want to think in principle about how we can conclude an agreement with Latin America that serves people in Austria, people in Europe and people in Latin America – for the basis of life, for prosperity and for the future. If that succeeds, we will be happy to agree. If that fails, we can still say no afterwards. We don't have to make a bad mood by lying before. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2021-02-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00085_d7e16082	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. Especially tourism, gastronomy, our entire travel industry, trade fairs and congress organizers have been in a real exceptional situation for a year because of the worldwide corona pandemic in which we are located. We have experienced quite a positive summer in the holiday regions, but the city hotel industry has unfortunately not been able to have such a one. In Austria, especially in urban tourism, we have an incredibly high share of international guests, of foreign guests, and the restriction of travel freedom is particularly striking in this industry. But we also have, and this is unfortunately the slightly negative part of the message, quite difficult months ahead of us. At present, we are also struggling with different mutations that are currently occurring, whether British mutation or those from South Africa; in addition, the Brazilian variant and the question of whether or not each vaccine is effective accordingly. All this has to cause us to set ourselves up for a rather difficult time, for difficult months. But we are, and I believe that is the important and very positive message, a popular, strong, good and high-quality holiday country and will definitely be able to get back to this old strength. Tourism is one of the most central economic sectors for us, but it is mainly a central job engine in Austria, especially in the rural regions, where businesses or industrial enterprises are very often lacking. For example, in 2018 in Austria, we generated an economic performance of almost 60 billion euros through tourism; Tourism and leisure sectors together contribute around 15 percent of economic performance to the GDP of the Austrian Republic. Especially as regards winter tourism, the number is very impressive: 50 percent of European winter sports tourism takes place in Austria – and of course we miss all this very painful this time. Unfortunately, due to the very high rate of infection in autumn, it was not possible to carry out this winter season this year as we normally know it. This was particularly painful with regard to the month of Feber, which is actually the most important for winter tourism, just as it is August for summer tourism, but travel freedom is heavily restricted throughout Europe. As Minister for Tourism, I am also in a very close exchange and contact with my European colleagues, and in all countries the situation of the industry is currently very desperate. For businesses, especially for me as a Minister of Tourism, this lack of planning and this lack of clear prognosis – at day X it will be like that – is currently the most painful. But we know – and we as the Federal Government are well aware of the responsibility – that planning security is necessary for the event economy, for the hotel industry and also for gastronomy. It takes lead times to drive up farms again and to accept bookings accordingly. Accordingly, we have agreed that together with the industry we maintain a very intensive exchange, also advise on how possible opening steps can be taken. We rely very massively on a comprehensive test concept. In recent months – and for this I am particularly grateful to the federal states in particular – we have developed comprehensive testing possibilities that can be tested daily by every Austrian and every Austrian. This should and will definitely be a bridge for us until the time when we have completely overcome the corona virus or anyone who wants a vaccination can get it. We are currently together with the industry, with the representatives of gastronomy, tourism, but above all with the event industry in the middle of a restart process. We will definitely expand our test programs that we have. ParlaMint-AT_2021-02-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00085_d7e16874	We're now on the 9th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mrs Kira Grünberg. Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_2021-02-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00085_d7e16904	Mr President. (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Dear Seers! First of all, I would like to thank the People’s Procuratorate for the report “No Chance of Work – The Reality of People with Disabilities”. This report states that the situation of people with disabilities on the labour market in Austria is not satisfactory and unacceptable. Unfortunately, I have to agree with God in part, because it is still more difficult for people with disabilities to find a job than it is for people with disabilities. People with disabilities will hardly have a chance in the regular labour market even in 2021. The situation has just worsened during the corona pandemic last year. Thanks to God, the labour market package has been able to absorb many things and to absorb more far-reaching negative effects, as this has helped to secure many jobs for people with disabilities. The Volksanwälte also shows in the report the problem of the current situation of people with disabilities in day-to-day structures, as people with disabilities, who work in day structures – and I use the word very consciously – are not socially insured. They are insured with their parents and therefore apply more or less than children; if a parent dies, they also receive orphan's orphan's pension. I think that this is simply no longer a matter of time and does not correspond to the UN Convention on the Rights of the Disabled. That is why the government programme has stated that we want to change this situation and that we must, in any case, change it. We want people working in day-to-day structures to get a wage and not a pocket money that they are socially insured themselves and to pay for themselves in the pension fund. [[Congratulations on ÖVP and Greens.]] A very crucial point is also the determination of the ability to work or of permanent incapacity for work. There are extremely great opportunities in Austria for people with disabilities to get more opportunities on the labour market. Austria has a highly differentiated and needs-based system to support people with disabilities with its professional assistance network, with the Neba services. These include youth coaching, job coaching, vocational training assistance, work assistance, and – now new – the business service, which enables people with disabilities to offer more opportunities in the labour market. The major problem now is that people with disabilities, who are classified as permanently incapacity for work, have no access to these services, and this must be changed. That is why we have already tabled a self-employed motion for a resolution on this matter, which is assigned to the Committee on Economic and Monetary Affairs and Industrial Policy, and I am very pleased to be once more deeply involved in the subject and to discuss all these issues in the Committee. The issue of the labour market and people with disabilities is a very complex one, because there are different types and severity of disability. That's why it usually needs very individual and human-made solutions. I am pleased to continue to turn on various screws to improve the situation of people with disabilities on the labour market. - Thank you.

In recent years, the public prosecutor's commissions have made 600 visits to institutions for people with disabilities and have received complaints there, namely from those concerned, from family members, from experts and, of course, from representatives of the institutions. It is important to me that it does not give the impression that these institutions are working badly, because the people there are actually very satisfied and have expressed themselves positively about the care. The staff in the workshops meet people with great appreciation and the public prosecutor's commissions have also called several institutions the best-practice examples. What is the problem now? - We've already heard: Austria has committed itself through the UN Disability Convention to grant people with disabilities a right to work, but such an inclusive labour market is currently not realized. In the year under review 2018, the employment rate of people with disabilities was 58.5 percent, with those with no disabilities being more than 20 percent. One problem is that young people with disabilities are often quickly qualified as unworkable. It is therefore difficult to find a place in the normal labour market, so that they work in workshops, where, as has already been said, there is no reward, but only a pocket money. Thus, they have no right to sickness benefit, social security under labour law, pension insurance or measures of the AMS relating to professional search. The persons concerned are dependent on the benefits of social assistance or on orphan pensions and the related social security claims. They thus feel that they are not fully integrated and not valued. We have – and this is what the government programme says – done to change that. It is a cross-sectional matter which affects all ministries, but also the countries and municipalities. That is why it is a great approach and not so easy to do. We, the government parties, have today tabled the self-employed motion for a resolution on the re-examination of the assessment of the working capacity of the The Committee on Economic and Monetary Affairs and Industrial Policy will be discussed. In the future, there will also be a permeability from the day-to-day locations to the general labour market. This is to be discussed. It is a first step and it is an important step towards improving access to the labour market for people with disabilities and their fair pay and insurance.

ParlaMint-AT_2021-02-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00085_d7e19646	People's attorney Mag. Achitz. - Here you go.
ParlaMint-AT_2021-02-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00085_d7e19667	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen. Thank you very much for treating this special report here and addressing this important issue. We also have the impression and we are very pleased that all speakers have signaled willingness to change the situation. Leave Let me summarize what we're talking about. People with disabilities in Austria are not sufficiently integrated into the labour market, there is no inclusive labour market, so they depend on employment in disabled workshops. At the same time, they often have no choice between individual institutions, but have to accept a certain offer. In these workshops they are not socially insured, they do not receive a wage, but only a pocket money, which is why they are in a forced situation. The persons concerned cannot take any action by the AMS to take part in the First Labour Market, there is no possibility of going into the sick or other workers' rights, the persons concerned depend on orphanage or social assistance. The provision of social assistance also makes it impossible to build up assets. There is no pension claim and therefore no old-age insurance. As a result, people of retirement age also live and work in many housing facilities and workshops for people with disabilities. All of this contradicts the UN Disability Convention and is unworthy of Austria. We therefore recommend abolishing the division of people with disabilities into workable and unworkable as possible, creating their own social security schemes and testing models of remuneration instead of the previous pocket money models. The motions for resolutions tabled today are a step in the right direction. A quick view is needed, legal changes are both in the National Council and in the Landstags. I hope and appeal to you to take these initiatives soon. Thank you very much.

There are public prosecutors, ombudsmen who are aware of this themselves – i.e. with their full-time employees – there are public prosecutors who serve experts in the various visits, and there are also constructions as we have them, that we order commissions, but then completely independent of their visits, write their reports of perception and carry out their activities. Let me also point out that it is in the People's Procuratorate Act: ‘The People’s Procuratorate may recall a member in writing and for reasons in advance’, on the basis of the Member’s request, on the basis of health problems, or if obligations relating to its function are grossly violated or permanently neglected, thus calling into question the independence of the exercise of the function. The People's Procuratorate itself has, moreover, extended these points in its own Rules of Procedure, expressly stating that such a withdrawal can only be carried out “after prior consultation of the Human Rights Advisory Council”. These findings – both in the People's Procuratorate Act and in the Rules of Procedure – do not change at all today. It is only made clear that the People's Procuratorate and its Commissions are becoming active as Parliament's body. I think that is also enough to ensure that clarity is created and that we do not have to persist in ambiguity. – Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-AT_2021-02-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00085_d7e21255	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! I don't think I need to repeat that we are in an economically difficult situation. It's known. I might want to repeat the numbers because they are repeated a little bit distorted. At the moment, this week, 440 000 people in unemployment, 70 000 people in training courses and 485 000 people for short-time work. Important: pre-registered, the billing is made afterwards. We know from experience that approximately 40 to 50 percent of these short-term work applications are not charged. That is why it is exaggerating to speak of a million people affected by short-term work and unemployment. At the same time – and this is important to me – we have seen a slight relaxation in recent weeks as regards unemployment. From last week to this week, the number of unemployed has decreased by 8 500. The week before that it was 7 500 and even the week before it was a few thousand. There is therefore a slight trend in relaxation which, of course, is linked to the opening steps in non-food trade and in the field of services close to the body. Nevertheless, the crisis in the labour market has not been overcome for a long time. It is therefore important that people can continue to count on our support and that all employees and businesses have planning security for the coming weeks and months. I referred briefly to the number of measures we have put in place in the morning. I would like to take two more measures, which are now also on the agenda. First: What are we doing? – Just about a year ago, at the beginning of the crisis, we decided to raise emergency aid to the level of unemployment benefit. The aim was to provide financial support to job seekers who have already been very difficult and who have made it undecided in this crisis. Therefore, we have prevented the disbursement of the increased emergency aid by the end of 2020 and have ensured an extension by the end of March. We therefore support 200 000 people every month who cannot move into emergency aid at this time. In addition, short work is our central labour market support instrument. More than a million people were thus saved from unemployment and remained in their jobs. I would like to stress once again that short work is unique in international comparison. There is no country in which short-time work is so generous and the rate of replacement paid by the State is as high for both businesses and employees as we do. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and MEPs of the Greens.]] Ladies and gentlemen! We have always stressed that as long as there is a regulatory closure in substantial areas of the economy, there must be corona short work in a generous form. That is why, after discussions with the social partners, with the social partners, with the various ministries concerned in recent weeks, we have now extended short work in the current form after the end of March for three months until the end of June. This means another three months in which businesses and their employees can rely on the success model of short-term work and be sure of support. What do the cornerstones look like? That too, I think, is important to repeat again. The net replacement rate remains at 80 or 90 percent. Working time can normally be reduced to up to 30 percent. In exceptional cases, if businesses are affected, for example, by official closures, working hours can be reduced to 0 percent. That's almost nowhere. In addition – and this is very important to me – further training will continue to be promoted. 60 percent of the cost of continuing training in firms for people in short-time work is reimbursed by the AMS. We have also provided financially: The framework for short work has been increased to 7 billion euros for 2021. That's enough for the time being. But if it's not enough, it's also clear that we're going to shoot. We have proved that we can respond quickly to the health development circumstances by setting out the phase four in concrete terms already, just under six weeks before the phase is over. So there is enough lead time for the employees, for the businesses and also for the AMS, for those who have to implement it. I would like to thank all those involved in this solution. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and MEPs of the Greens.]] At the same time, however, I would like to stress that while short-time work – and we have been in the deepest crisis since the Second World War as far as the economy is concerned – is a very important tool for ensuring employment, it is not a permanent instrument in this form, it is an instrument to compensate for this under-exploitation. Therefore, if the health development and the situation on the labour market allow it, we want to do so from 1. In July, a step-by-step exit from short-time work will begin in the current form. There will also be a short work, but the current form needs to be phased out. This is the requirement that, after the pandemic, the dynamics in the labour market will change as we need it, and that, in the upsurge that will come after the pandemic, we can create employment again and succeed in the labour market. Thank you.

They often need intensive medical care and they also need to be breathed more often. Of course, pregnancy in itself often means an increased risk of diabetes or high blood pressure, just like the Covid-19 high-risk group in itself. In spring 2020, it was still believed that Corona had no special effects during pregnancy, but American studies have already shown that there are cases in which the virus was transferred to the unborn, that babies in the placenta are under-supplyed – with the resulting late sequences – and that it is reported in a number of cases of miscarriages and single births. Therefore, preventive measures should be taken to protect pregnant mothers from infections. On the one hand, it is important to reduce social contacts – we know that – but on the other hand also the customer contacts. This, of course, concerns the area of services close to the body, in which no other protection measures can be taken for pregnant women, but also other services. Let's think about the trade: Homeoffice is difficult to find and it is difficult to reduce customer contacts. It is our job to protect precisely those who cannot do it themselves, and this clearly includes our unborn children. We will not impose unnecessary dangers on future generations and therefore we will extend early maternity protection until the end of June.

If I have a reproduction factor of 0.3 or 0.4 times higher, then the total number of reproductions and thus the number of infections generally increases. If we have as at present a reproductive factor of 1.1, that means that of 100 people who have an infection, 110 others are infected. This increases the curve, and this is of course worrying. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Belakovich.]] Pressure on infection increases. We take five concrete measures, which are far from disputed among all experts. We now have a very precise knowledge and know that in the east of Austria, in Vienna, for example, the proportion of mutations of the British virus is now more than 50 percent, similar in Lower Austria, in Burgenland now well over 70 percent. The implementation of this so-called recovery period is carried out on the one hand under scientific supervision, on the other hand, the Ministry – you, Minister – has announced that there will be an additional study that takes into account sexually transmitted diseases and also the health impact assessment, and that is very important. We've never been that far, because the killing argument has always been that we don't have scientific studies. That means we're not in the end – that's true. Thank you for the debate that we keep leading to until we have the solution once again that this blood donation ban will fall. For this we continue to fight with the community, with the SPÖ, with NEOS, with those concerned, because no one can understand that in Austria one excludes a certain group from the blood donation, but everyone will understand that, of course, because of the blood security, we ask certain questions, namely, all persons – whether they have a risk behaviour that could lead to exclusion. That is, we continue to insist, the fight for a completely non-discrimination-free blood donation continues. The good thing about this is that we now have at least an adequate contact. Thank you.

This is, of course, relevant and important in many respects in the crisis: on the one hand, because it is necessary to offer it in as many cases as possible, if workers would like to have it, – because it is also a positive aspect if you show more flexibility – but on the other hand, because we can continue to limit social contacts in order to limit the spread of the virus as possible. The tax measures in detail: It is about the provision by the employer of the necessary digital work resources, which does not create a taxable subject-matter; 3 euros per day can be transmitted to workers for a maximum of 100 days per year, without any tax attack. That is to say, this measure. is limited to 2023 in order to see if it has had the appropriate benefit or effect, or if it needs to be improved somewhere. This, contrary to national opinion, helps small and medium-sized enterprises in particular: About 77 percent of applicants are small companies with an annual turnover of up to 700 000 euros. A total of EUR 6.5 billion was the maximum amount of support or reduction in prepayments, which is currently EUR 5.3 billion. This support was originally planned by the beginning of this year, and we extended it in December to the end of the first quarter. Because of the fact that the crisis through mutations and other developments will unfortunately not be over as quickly as we would all wish, we have said that we should also extend these measures once again, by 30. June. I can say to Mr Kassager: The 2 million euro basic salary has nothing to do with this National Commission. This National Commission aims to implement international humanitarian law, where the departments, experts, experts and non-governmental organisations come together. In most other countries, these Commissions have a legal basis, in Austria there has not yet been it. This Commission will now be put on its feet legally, and there will also be an international conference in Vienna. On the one hand, this is about the agreement between the humanitarian aid programmes we are making, but also about essential areas of international humanitarian law, such as our initiatives in the field of disarmament or the prohibition of particularly dangerous weapons. I think basic funding is essential – and it is, so to speak, a recognition of what the Red Cross has done here in the past, what it will do as a partner in the future. It puts this cooperation on stable and powerful legs and secures it. I believe that both agenda items in summary reflect the traditional commitment we have in Austria and which we have rightly always been proud of, for the strengthening of humanitarian law, for dialogue, for Vienna as a place of dialogue and office. That is why I hope for the widest possible approval. – Thank you very much.

The UNHCR says that there were about 80 million refugees in 2019, of which 26 million are seeking protection from conflicts, persecution and serious human rights violations and are leaving their home country. In countries which were considered to be somewhat stable and which are, like the US, conflicts arise, which may have been reinforced by Corona, but at least by a current in society, which is sometimes difficult to understand. Who would have thought that the heart of democracy, the Capitol in Washington, would be stormed by an uprooted population? This shows that there is great unrest and tension in society worldwide, and at the same time increases the longing that there are places that are quite stable. Places in which the United Nations has its seat are considered to be such – for example, New York, Geneva, but also Vienna. Vienna has been a location of the United Nations for 40 years, making it an important hub between East and West, North and South, a popular meeting place. In this light I see the law of office, because I believe that it is especially important in such troubled times that there are places where you can meet peacefully and maintain the exchange, which ultimately benefits the whole world. They have heard that more than 50 international organisations have their headquarters here, to revive important organizations such as the International Atomic Energy Authority, which may also be a support for the efforts, the nuclear deal about Iran, which has been concluded in Vienna, so to speak. It would also be worth mentioning activities in the area of human rights, renewable energy, some of the external offices of international organisations, which are important, which show that Vienna and Austria are to be such a location and are to be expanded as such. We hope that we can make a contribution to make our world a lot more peaceful and liveworthy. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2021-02-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00085_d7e30189	Mr President. Mrs. Minister! Minister! Artificial intelligence has long arrived in society and is very advanced, especially in the military field under enormous development costs. Precise military systems, such as combat drones, are already used specifically in military conflicts. As a recent example, the conflict in Bergkarabach between Armenia and Azerbaijan was mentioned. As never before, artificial intelligence has clearly decided the outcome of the military dispute. It is to be observed that many armies are forcing their programmes to build autonomous weapons systems and are increasingly focusing on the so-called integration of robotic Combat systems. It is a development that can determine and shape future conflicts. The United Nations must therefore implement a international ban on fully autonomous weapons systems. The decision on a person's life and death must under no circumstances be delegated to algorithms. Therefore, such autonomous automatic weapons systems should not be eligible under the European Defence Fund. Austria, together with Chile and Brazil in 2018, has already addressed the safeguarding of human control in critical functions of autonomous weapons systems and has declared itself Decided against killer robots. On this occasion, however, we also need to address conventional arms supplies more clearly. I only mention the example of Libya, where a proxy war is raging between Söldnerarmen, between Russia and Turkey. Mein Ladies and gentlemen, a weapon system designed to select and attack objectives independently without human control is not only to be rejected, such a system must be prohibited. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] This is particularly true in view of the fact that such weapons systems could also be used domestically against their own population. Without human control and the possibility of effective intervention at every stage of action, wars of the future become computer games with real destruction unimaginable extent. In Austria as a peace-loving country, the development, production, procurement, Prohibition and sale, import, export and transit as well as possession of fully autonomous weapons and weapons systems are prohibited. Artificial intelligence is neither good nor evil. It must be used for the benefit of people. Artificial intelligence can and may support the rule of law and democracy. It can even secure peace and speed up disarmament. Wrongly used, however, progressive artificial intelligence can also lead the world into the abyss. There lies the high responsibility of politics and, in particular, of democracies. Thank you.

He asks, for example, that a state of our size should carry out its own national defence at all. He also makes fun of the fact that Turkey is actually hindering our contribution to the peace partnership, which is because of our international attitude to Turkey. That's what I can do. If that's the private opinion of a law historian, then I'm good and right, then that's what it's supposed to be. It wasn't like that. Your cabinet has continued to work, your Secretary-General has then made it clear to this group leader that this was not the private opinion of the author of this essay, but that it is of course the opinion of the department, that is your opinion. I am concerned, Mrs. Minister, that you have considered the “Austrian military magazine” to be an extra-party, internationally recognized magazine, since party-political propaganda has been misused from the side of your cabinet. It would have been better, Mrs. Minister, if you had made it clear that this is a private opinion of a writer and not the opinion of the Republic, and thus made it clear that what you have issued as a muzzler for the other staff should also apply to the staff closer to your cabinet or even to the members of the cabinet. The “Austrian Military Journal” has so far been a recognized magazine, I have mentioned it, and has not been damaged by party policy, but in this case it has actually happened with this special issue – and that is a pity. Mrs. Minister, you should consider whether you have occasionally applied this muzzle basket, which you have issued for the general staff, for your cabinet, and at all times make sure in your cabinet that these chatterings about things that are important to the Republic are set up. Thank you very much.
